Those guards mutate a\n95\t// thread-local span stack. The previous code kept one alive across the\n96\t// claim await; when Tokio resumed the task on a different worker thread,\n97\t// dropping the guard there could not remove the entry left on the original\n98\t// thread. The lifecycle guard had the same cross-await problem. One\n99\t// `bead.claim` plus one `bead.lifecycle` leaked per cycle. Because the fmt\n100\t// layer re-serializes the whole span stack on every event, output grew\n101\t// quadratically: measured at 18 deep / 4,983-byte lines early and 2,488 deep\n102\t// / 629,829-byte lines late, reaching ~159 GB/hr and filling a 444 GB disk.\n103\t// See bf-3uj6i.\n104\t//\n105\t// `.instrument()` attaches the span to the future correctly, so\n106\t// `Span::current()` inside `claim_one` (claim/mod.rs records\n107\t// needle.claim.result there) still resolves to this span.\n108\t```\n109\t\n110\t#### 2.1.2 Claim Auto Usage\n111\t\n112\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~1335-1343\n113\t\n114\t```rust\n115\tlet strand = \"auto\";\n116\tlet claim = self.claimer.claim_auto(&self.qualified_id(), strand).await;\n117\t```\n118\t\n119\t**Observation:** ⚠️ **NO SPAN CONTEXT** - `claim_auto` is called without an explicit span wrapper\n120\t\n121\t**Risk:** Low - The call inherits the current span context (likely `strand.auto` or similar from the strand evaluation)\n122\t\n123\t### 2.2 Lifecycle Span Creation\n124\t\n125\tAfter a successful claim, the worker creates a `bead.lifecycle` span:\n126\t\n127\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~1729-1743\n128\t\n129\t```rust\n130\tlet lifecycle_span = tracing::info_span!(\n131\t    \"bead.lifecycle\",\n132\t    needle.bead.id = %self.current_bead.as_ref().map(|b| b.id.as_ref()).unwrap_or(\"unknown\"),\n133\t    needle.bead.priority = bead_priority.unwrap_or(0),\n134\t    needle.bead.title_hash = %bead_title_hash.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"unknown\"),\n135\t    needle.bead.outcome = tracing::field::Empty, // Will be set on completion\n136\t);\n137\tself.bead_lifecycle_span = Some(lifecycle_span);\n138\t```\n139\t\n140\t**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - Span is stored, not entered. Used later with `.instrument()`\n141\t\n142\t### 2.3 Lifecycle Span Usage\n143\t\n144\tThe lifecycle span is used with `.instrument()` for async operations and `in_scope()` for sync operations:\n145\t\n146\t#### 2.3.1 Async Operations with `.instrument()`\n147\t\n148\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~956-966\n149\t\n150\t```rust\n151\tWorkerState::Building => self.do_build().instrument(lifecycle_span.clone()).await?,\n152\tWorkerState::Prompting => {\n153\t    self.do_prompt()\n154\t        .instrument(lifecycle_span.clone())\n155\t        .await?\n156\t}\n157\tWorkerState::Executing => self.do_execute().instrument(lifecycle_span.clone()).await?,\n158\tWorkerState::Handling => self.do_handle().instrument(lifecycle_span.clone()).await?,\n159\t```\n160\t\n161\t**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - All async operations use `.instrument()`\n162\t\n163\t#### 2.3.2 Sync Operations with `.in_scope()`\n164\t\n165\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~969\n166\t\n167\t```rust\n168\tWorkerState::Logging => lifecycle_span.in_scope(|| self.do_log())?,\n169\t```\n170\t\n171\t**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - `do_log()` is synchronous (no await points)\n172\t\n173\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~5039-5044 (test code)\n174\t\n175\t```rust\n176\tlifecycle_span.in_scope(|| {\n177\t    tracing::info!(cycle, \"claim-cycle-depth-probe\");\n178\t});\n179\t\n180\tlifecycle_span.in_scope(|| worker.do_log()).unwrap();\n181\t```\n182\t\n183\t**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - All `in_scope()` closures are synchronous\n184\t\n185\t### 2.4 Span Documentation Comments\n186\t\n187\tThe worker module includes explicit documentation about span safety:\n188\t\n189\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~395\n190\t\n191\t```rust\n192\t/// The current bead lifecycle span. Created when a bead is claimed and\n193\t/// remains active for the entire bead processing pipeline.\n194\t///\n195\t/// This must remain a `Span`, not an `EnteredSpan`: entered guards mutate a\n196\t/// thread-local span stack and are NOT safe to hold across `.await` points.\n197\t/// The span is attached to async operations via `.instrument()` instead.\n198\t```\n199\t\n200\t**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~23\n201\t\n202\t```rust\n203\t// Needed for `.instrument()` — attaches a span to a future instead of holding an\n204\t// `Entered` guard across `.await`, which is unsound and leaked spans (bf-3uj6i).\n205\t```\n206\t\n207\t---\n208\t\n209\t## 3. Span Module (`src/span/mod.rs`)\n210\t\n211\t### 3.1 ScopeGuard Implementation\n212\t\n213\tThe `ScopeGuard` type is explicitly documented as **UNSAFE to use across await points**:\n214\t\n215\t**Location:** `src/span/mod.rs` ~143-189\n216\t\n217\t```rust\n218\t/// RAII guard for LIFO-safe span scoping.\n219\t///\n220\t/// When dropped, the guard explicitly closes the span, ensuring proper\n221\t/// unwinding even across early returns or errors. Cross-Await Safety Verification\n301\t\n302\t### 5.1 Pattern Analysis\n303\t\n304\tThe claim path follows two patterns for span handling:\n305\t\n306\t#### Pattern 1: `.instrument()` for Async Operations ✅\n307\t\n308\t```rust\n309\tlet span = tracing::info_span!(\"operation\");\n310\tasync_operation().instrument(span).await\n311\t```\n312\t\n313\t**Used in:**\n314\t- `worker::do_claim()` - claim_one call\n315\t- `worker::do_build()` - build operations\n316\t- `worker::do_execute()` - execute operations\n317\t- `worker::do_handle()` - handle operations\n318\t- `strand::select()` - strand evaluation\n319\t\n320\t#### Pattern 2: `in_scope()` for Sync Operations ✅\n321\t\n322\t```rust\n323\tspan.in_scope(|| {\n324\t    // synchronous work only\n325\t})\n326\t```\n327\t\n328\t**Used in:**\n329\t- `worker::do_log()` - logging is synchronous\n330\t- `strand::select()` - bookkeeping after await completes\n331\t\n332\t### 5.2 Anti-Pattern Detection\n333\t\n334\tThe following anti-patterns were searched for and **NOT FOUND** in the claim path:\n335\t\n336\t❌ Holding an `Entered` guard across an `.await` point  \n337\t❌ Using `ScopeGuard` across an `.await` point  \n338\t❌ Calling `span.enter()` before an `.await` without dropping before  \n339\t❌ Storing an `EnteredSpan` in a struct field  \n340\t\n341\t---\n342\t\n343\t## 6. Worker Module (`src/worker/mod.rs`)\n\n### 2.1 Span Creation in Claim Path\n\nThe worker creates spans using `tracing::info_span!()` and attaches them to futures with `.instrument()`.\n\n#### 2.1.1 Claim Span Creation (`do_claim()`)\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~1691-1708\n\n```rust\nlet claim_span = tracing::info_span!(\n    \"bead.claim\",\n    needle.bead.id = %bead_id.as_ref(),\n    needle.claim.retry_number = tracing::field::Empty,\n    needle.claim.result = tracing::field::Empty,\n);\n\nlet claim = self\n    .claimer\n    .claim_one(&bead_id, &self.qualified_id(), &exclusions, Some(strand))\n    .instrument(claim_span.clone())\n    .await?;\n```\n\n**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - Uses `.instrument()` to attach span to future, no guard held\n\n**Documentation in Code:**\n```rust\n// Do NOT hold an `Entered`/`EnteredSpan` guard here. Those guards mutate a\n// thread-local span stack. The previous code kept one alive across the\n// claim await; when Tokio resumed the task on a different worker thread,\n// dropping the guard there could not remove the entry left on the original\n// thread. The lifecycle guard had the same cross-await problem. One\n// `bead.claim` plus one `bead.lifecycle` leaked per cycle. Because the fmt\n// layer re-serializes the whole span stack on every event, output grew\n// quadratically: measured at 18 deep / 4,983-byte lines early and 2,488 deep\n// / 629,829-byte lines late, reaching ~159 GB/hr and filling a 444 GB disk.\n// See bf-3uj6i.\n//\n// `.instrument()` attaches the span to the future correctly, so\n// `Span::current()` inside `claim_one` (claim/mod.rs records\n// needle.claim.result there) still resolves to this span.\n```\n\n#### 2.1.2 Claim Auto Usage\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~1335-1343\n\n```rust\nlet strand = \"auto\";\nlet claim = self.claimer.claim_auto(&self.qualified_id(), strand).await;\n```\n\n**Observation:** ⚠️ **NO SPAN CONTEXT** - `claim_auto` is called without an explicit span wrapper\n\n**Risk:** Low - The call inherits the current span context (likely `strand.auto` or similar from the strand evaluation)\n\n### 2.2 Lifecycle Span Creation\n\nAfter a successful claim, the worker creates a `bead.lifecycle` span:\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~1729-1743\n\n```rust\nlet lifecycle_span = tracing::info_span!(\n    \"bead.lifecycle\",\n    needle.bead.id = %self.current_bead.as_ref().map(|b| b.id.as_ref()).unwrap_or(\"unknown\"),\n    needle.bead.priority = bead_priority.unwrap_or(0),\n    needle.bead.title_hash = %bead_title_hash.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"unknown\"),\n    needle.bead.outcome = tracing::field::Empty, // Will be set on completion\n);\nself.bead_lifecycle_span = Some(lifecycle_span);\n```\n\n**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - Span is stored, not entered. Used later with `.instrument()`\n\n### 2.3 Lifecycle Span Usage\n\nThe lifecycle span is used with `.instrument()` for async operations and `in_scope()` for sync operations:\n\n#### 2.3.1 Async Operations with `.instrument()`\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~956-966\n\n```rust\nWorkerState::Building => self.do_build().instrument(lifecycle_span.clone()).await?,\nWorkerState::Prompting => {\n    self.do_prompt()\n        .instrument(lifecycle_span.clone())\n        .await?\n}\nWorkerState::Executing => self.do_execute().instrument(lifecycle_span.clone()).await?,\nWorkerState::Handling => self.do_handle().instrument(lifecycle_span.clone()).await?,\n```\n\n**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - All async operations use `.instrument()`\n\n#### 2.3.2 Sync Operations with `.in_scope()`\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~969\n\n```rust\nWorkerState::Logging => lifecycle_span.in_scope(|| self.do_log())?,\n```\n\n**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - `do_log()` is synchronous (no await points)\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~5039-5044 (test code)\n\n```rust\nlifecycle_span.in_scope(|| {\n    tracing::info!(cycle, \"claim-cycle-depth-probe\");\n});\n\nlifecycle_span.in_scope(|| worker.do_log()).unwrap();\n```\n\n**Safety:** ✅ **SAFE** - All `in_scope()` closures are synchronous\n\n### 2.4 Span Documentation Comments\n\nThe worker module includes explicit documentation about span safety:\n\n**Location:** `src/worker/mod.rs` ~395\n\n```rust\n/// The current bead lifecycle span. Span Module (`src/span/mod.rs`)\n\n### 3.1 ScopeGuard Implementation\n\nThe `ScopeGuard` type is explicitly documented as **UNSAFE to use across await points**:\n\n**Location:** `src/span/mod.rs` ~143-189\n\n```rust\n/// RAII guard for LIFO-safe span scoping.\n///\n/// When dropped, the guard explicitly closes the span, ensuring proper\n/// unwinding even across early returns or errors. This ensures LIFO\n/// (Last-In-First-Out) compliance by using RAII to guarantee spans unwind\n/// in reverse order.\n///\n/// # Safety\n///\n/// This guard must NOT be stored across an `.await` point.
